Need make final code not too complex but robust. Let's design final program with the following components:
1. Constants, time budget.
2. Score function internal `_score(a)` using FFT or direct, but also an optional true score wrapper `_evaluate(a)` that calls global evaluate_sequence if available, else internal. Use internal for optimization loops, true for periodic candidate checks.
3. Convolution `_convolution(a)` using FFT for n>=256, direct for smaller. Ensure nonnegative, finite.
4. `_softmax_weights(c, tau, top_k=None)`: If top_k, set weights on top peaks exp; else all. For gradient.
5. `_projected_subgrad_step(a, lr, tau, top_k)`:
   - c = convolution(a)
   - weights = softmax(c, tau, topK)
   - grad = _autograd(a, weights)
   - grad -= mean
   - Use multiplicative update: `log_ratio = np.exp(-lr * grad / scale)`, a = a * log_ratio, normalize. Need avoid underflow. Since grad has mean 0, scale maybe max(abs(grad)) or std. Use `lr * grad / grad_max` with exp in [-1,1]. This is stable.
   Actually gradient values roughly of order a; lr can be many. Use step:
   ```
   g = grad - grad.mean()
   gscale = np.max(np.abs(g)) + 1e-300
   a = a * np.exp(-lr * g / gscale)
   a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
   a *= 1/a.sum()
   ```
   This is like multiplicative weight with step lr in [0, ~2]. It will move mass away from high gradient (places contributing to peak) and toward low. For exact problem, it may flatten.

FFT convolution implementation:
```
def _convolution_full(a):
    n=len(a)
    if n < 256:
       return np.convolve(a,a)
    N=1
    m=2*n-1
    while N < m: N <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
    c=c[:m]
    c[np.abs(c) < 1e-12*max(1, c.max())] = 0? maybe not
    return c
```
For n=2048 N=4096; memory fine. For n=10000 N=32768; maybe okay but many evals slow. We'll cap lengths to maybe 4096.

def _grad_from_weights(a, w):
    n=len(a)
    if n < 256:
       grad=np.zeros(n)
       # direct: for i in range(n): grad[i] = 2*np.dot(a, w[i:i+n]) maybe O(n^2); for small ok
       for i ...
    else:
       r=a[::-1].copy()
       # Need N >= len(w)+n-1
       m=3*n-2
       N=1
       while N<m: N <<=1
       fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
       fr=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
       z=np.fft.irfft(fw*fr,N)
       grad = 2*z[n-1:2*n-1]
    return grad
```
For direct small, can use `np.dot(a, w[i:i+n])` each i O(n^2) for n=256 -> 65k, okay.

def _weights_from_conv(c, tau=None, top_k=0):
    if tau is None:
       mstar=np.argmax(c)
       # Could add small mass to all? No hard.
       w=np.zeros(len(c)); w[mstar]=1.0
    else:
       if top_k and top_k < len(c):
           # top_k highest peaks
           idx=np.argpartition(c, -top_k)[-top_k:]
           vals=c[idx]
           # set others 0
       else:
           idx=np.arange(len(c)); vals=c
       vals = vals - vals.max()
       # tau maybe large; scale to avoid underflow. We can normalize by sum exp(tau*vals).
       e=np.exp(tau*vals)
       s=e.sum()
       if s==0 or not finite: w uniform over top? else w=e/s
       if top_k: w_full zeros; w_full[idx]=w
    return w
```

def _sanitize(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)

```
def _sum_normalize(a, target=1.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        # uniform positive
        n = len(a)
        if n <=0: return np.ones(1)
        return np.full(n, target/float(n))
    if s < target and s < 0.01:
        # scale up maybe
    a = a * (target/s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    # renormalize after clipping? If clipping affects sum, repeat
    s2 = a.sum()
    if s2 > 1e-300 and abs(s2-target)>1e-9:
        a = a * (target/s2)
    return a
```
But if a has value >1000 after scaling, clip and renorm may make sum <target. We can simply target=1, values small; no clip issue. For `height_sequence_1` values maybe >1000, but normalize to 1 after clip. Use:
```
def _normalize(a, target=1.0):

    a = a * (target/s)
    a = np.minimum(a, 1000.0)
    s2 = a.sum()
    if s2 <=0: ...

    a = np.minimum(a,1000.0)
    return a
```

`_score_internal(a)`:
```
def _score_internal(a):
    a = _sanitize(a)
    n=len(a)
    if n==0: return inf
    S=float(a.sum())
    if S < 0.01: return inf
    c = _conv(a)
    mb = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mb) or mb <0: mb=0? 
    return float(2.0*n*mb/(S*S))
```

`_conv(a)` choose direct vs FFT. Need if n<384 direct to avoid FFT overhead? Direct np.convolve for n=512 262k, fine. FFT for >512.
```
def _convolution(a):
    n=a.size
    if n < 384:
        c = np.convolve(a,a, mode='full')
        # clean small negatives
        if c.size and c.max() > 0:
           tol = 1e-12 * c.max()
           c[c < tol] = 0? But for true max, clipping small negative okay. If legitimate small values, not matter.
        return c
    m = 2*n-1
    N=1
    while N < m: N <<=1
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)
    c=c[:m]
    # Due to roundoff, may have negative small; max unaffected if positive. But for gradient weights, negative small can cause exponential small. Set negatives to 0.
    c = np.maximum(c,0.0)
    return c
```
For n=1, m=1, N=1? rfft length 1 works? fa*fa, irfft length1. okay. But direct for small.

`_fft_grad(a,w)`:
```
def _gradient_from_weights(a,w):
    n=a.size
    if n==1: return np.array([2.0*w[0]*a[0]])? w len1.
    if n < 384:
       grad=np.empty(n)
       # direct via slices and dot
       for i in range(n):
           grad[i]=2.0*float(np.dot(a, w[i:i+n]))
       return grad
    m = len(w)+n-1  # 3n-2
    N=1
    while N < m: N <<=1
    r = a[::-1].copy()
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    fr=np.fft.rfft(r,N)
    z=np.fft.irfft(fw*fr,N)
    grad = 2.0 * z[n-1:2*n-1]
    # clean
    grad[~np.isfinite(grad)] = 0
    return grad
```
Direct loop for n=383: 146k dot length ~383? Actually each dot n, O(n^2)=146k, okay. But Python loop 383 each, fine. For n=512 use FFT.

Could optimize direct with `np.convolve`? We derived not simple. Could use FFT for >=128. Let's use direct threshold 128 to reduce Python loops. For n=256, direct 65k multiplications but Python loop 256, okay. Use threshold 160.

`_hard_gradient(a, mstar)`:
```
def _hard_gradient(a, mstar):
    n=a.size; grad=np.zeros(n)
    lo=max(0, mstar-n+1); hi=min(n-1,mstar)
    if lo<=hi:
       grad[lo:hi+1] = 2.0 * a[mstar-hi:mstar-lo+1][::-1]?
```
Need correct: for j in lo..hi, grad[j]=2*a[mstar-j]. Let idx = mstar - j; as j increases idx decreases. We can assign vector:
```

`_weights(c, tau, top_k)`:
```
def _weights_from_conv(c, tau=None, top_k=0):
    L=c.size
    if tau is None:
       w=np.zeros(L); m=int(np.argmax(c)); w[m]=1.0; return w
    if top_k and top_k < L:
       # if top_k <=0 all
       idx = np.argpartition(c, L-top_k)[-top_k:] if top_k < L else np.arange(L)
       vals = c[idx]
       vmax = vals.max()
       e = np.exp(tau*(vals-vmax))
       s = e.sum()
       if not finite or s<=0: e = np.ones_like(idx).astype(float); s=e.sum()
       wloc=e/s
       w=np.zeros(L); w[idx]=wloc
    else:
       vals=c
       vmax=vals.max()
       # if tau small (e.g. 1) weights broad. To avoid underflow, okay.
       e=np.exp(tau*(vals-vmax))
       s=e.sum()
       if not finite or s<=0:
          # if overflow handled; if all zero, focus argmax
          w=np.zeros(L); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1
       else: w=e/s
    return w
```
If tau large and c has multiple equal peaks, exp works. `tau` maybe scaled by? c values ~1/n, so tau*diff. If n=512, c differences maybe 1e-6? Need tau large like 1e6 to focus? Wait c values ~0.002; differences near peak maybe ~1e-5. `tau=50` gives exp(0.0005) nearly uniform; no. Previous used tau=20-50 for normalized sum sqrt(2n) where c~2, differences ~0.01; okay. With sum=1, c scaled by 1/(2n)? Actually previous sum sqrt(2n), S^2=2n, uniform c=2. Now sum=1, c=2/(2n)=1/n. Differences scale 1/n. So tau must be multiplied by n to have same effect. We can instead normalize c for weights by its max or std. Better: use normalized weights: `v = c / c.max()` or `v = (c - c.mean()) / std`. To focus, use `tau` on relative values. Compute `c_centered = c - c.max() <=0`, scale by `c.max()` or peak. `w = exp(beta * (c / max_c - 1))`. Dimensionsless. For uniform c max=1/n, offpeak near ~1/n*(1 - |lag|/n). Difference fraction ~|lag|/n. beta=50 gives enough. Good. So in weights: use `if vmax > 0: x=tau*(vals/vmax - 1.0)` not `tau*(vals-vmax)`. But if values vary large, fraction. Or use `tau * (vals - mean)/std`? Need robust.

Objective max peak; soft weights should emphasize peaks proportionally. Let `u = vals / (vmax + eps) - 1` in [-1,0]. Then `exp(beta * u)`. beta=100 means peak weight e^0, half if half lower. Good. For top_k, use all top values relative to max.

`_subgrad_step(a, lr, tau, top_k)`:
- compute c = _convolution(a) (we can pass c to avoid recompute in loop)
- if tau is None: weights hard, use _hard_gradient with argmax(c) for speed.
- else weights = _weights_from_conv(c, tau, top_k); grad = _gradient_from_weights(a, weights)
- center: g = grad - grad.mean()
- scale: gscale = max(abs(g)); if <=0 return a
- multiplicative update: `ratio = np.exp(-lr * g / gscale)`; `a2 = a * ratio`; clip; normalize.
But if lr too large, ratio underflow to 0 for many; normalize recovers. If all zero, reset to a or uniform.

Should we use projected gradient instead? Multiplicative can get stuck with zeros if a has zero and ratio infinite? For zero entries, a*r=0; cannot add mass to zeros because multiplicative update only scales existing positives. If a has zeros, they remain zero. This can be bad if starting with zeros. We need allow mass transfer into zeros. Use additive gradient step then projection, or add small floor. To avoid permanent zeros, initialize with positive floor (e.g. all >1e-8). During multiplicative, if a_i tiny, ratio large may increase but if exactly zero remains zero. We can add floor after update: `a = np.maximum(a, min_val)` then normalize. But floor can counteract sparsity. For nonnegative optimization, all uniform likely; but if hidden optimum sparse? Need allow zeros. Additive step can create negatives then clipped to zero, but zero can become positive if gradient negative? If a_i=0, gradient from convolution maybe 0 for some? Could be positive if contributes to peak? Not if zero. It may never become positive. To allow exploring zeros, use occasional random perturbation floor. Or use additive projected gradient:
```
a2 = a - lr*g/gscale
a2 = np.maximum(a2, 0); normalize.
```
Starting positive can become zero but later if gradient negative (low contribution) and a is zero, update a-(-)=positive, so can re-enter. Good. Use additive step with small learning rate. Maybe combine: additive for flexibility, but nonnegative. Choose `lr` in [0.01,0.5]. Since g/gscale in [-1,1], a2 may negative; clip. This can move mass. For exact min, uniform stable.

Which is better? Multiplicative tends to preserve positivity but cannot increase zeros. Additive can oscillate. We can use a hybrid: `a2 = a * np.exp(-alpha*g/gscale)` for positive, then add small noise/floor and normalize. Or use additive with adaptive step and floor 1e-12.

    a = _normalize(np.array(a0, dtype=float), 1.0)

    cur = _score_from_conv(a,c)
    best_arr=a.copy(); best_score=cur; best_true=_true_score(a) maybe True at start? Could be expensive but okay for each start? Maybe too many. We can set best_true = _true_score(a) once. If many starts ~20, true eval each start length 512 ~262k, 20*0.001=0.02s, fine. For 2048 4M, 20*0.05=1s, ok.

    min_step=0.02; max_step=0.35
    for it in range(1000000):
       if time.monotonic() > end-2: break

       frac = min(1.0, it / max(1, min(max_iters, 1000)))
       step = max_step * (1 - 0.7*frac) + min_step

       else compute grad

Let's design propose:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 10.0)

    # candidate pool: list of (true_score, internal_score, n, arr)
    pool=[]
    best_true=inf; best_seq=None

    # get height_sequence_1
    hs_raw = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    hs = _sanitize(hs_raw) if hs_raw is not None else None

    # Generate starts
    starts = _generate_starts(rng, hs, max_n=2048)
    # Evaluate each start true and internal, add to pool if finite.
    for arr in starts:
       arr = _normalize(arr,1.0)
       inner = _score_internal(arr)
       if not finite: continue
       true = _true_score(arr)
       pool.append((true, inner, arr))
       if true < best_true: best_true=true; best_seq=arr.copy()
       if time left small break

    # Sort pool by true score then internal. Use top-level key no lambda.
    pool.sort(key=_pool_key)

    # If budget left, optimize top starts. Assign time slice based on n and rank.
    spent=0
    for rank, (true, inner, arr) in enumerate(pool):
       remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
       if remaining <= 60: break
       n = len(arr)
       # slice = min(remaining*0.5? , by n)
       if n <= 256: slice = 180
       elif n <= 512: slice = 240
       elif n <= 1024: slice = 300
       else: slice = 200
       if rank == 0: slice = min(slice + 120, max(120, remaining*0.6))
       slice = min(slice, remaining - 60)
       if slice < 30: continue
       # choose mode based on rank and n
       mode = 'soft' if rank%2==0 else 'hard' maybe topk cycles.
       arr2, sc2, = _optimize_subgrad(arr, slice, mode=mode, rng=rng, tau_start=20, tau_end=150)
       true2 = _true_score(arr2)
       if true2 < best_true - 1e-12:
           best_true=true2; best_seq=arr2.copy(); print
       # Also consider arr2 if true slightly worse but internal? no.
       # Optionally if n<=512 and time left, do LP refinement on best_seq or arr2.
       if n <= 768 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 120:
           arr_lp = _lp_refine(best_seq, min(120, deadline-time.monotonic()), rng)
           true_lp = _true_score(arr_lp)
           if true_lp < best_true: update
    # Final refinement on best_seq with remaining time
    if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 30:
       slice = remaining - 30
       arr3, sc3 = _optimize_subgrad(best_seq, slice, mode='mixed', rng=rng, tau_start=10, tau_end=80)
       true3 = _true_score(arr3)
       if true3 < best_true: update
    # return best_seq (sanitized) or uniform 512
    if best_seq is None: best_seq = np.ones(512)
    best_seq = _normalize(best_seq, 1.0)
    # final list
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```

### Detailed helper functions

No nested, no lambda. Let's outline all top-level:

```
import numpy as np
import time
try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None
```
Can import scipy? allowed. If not, linprog None.

#### `_clean_array(a)`
```
def _clean_array(a):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.reshape(-1)
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
    return arr
```

#### `_normalize(a, target=1.0)`
```
def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    arr = _clean_array(a)
    if arr is None or arr.size == 0:
        return np.full(1, float(target), dtype=np.float64)
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(arr.size, target / float(arr.size), dtype=np.float64)
    arr = arr * (target / s)
    arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(arr.sum())
    if s2 > 1e-300:
        arr = arr * (target / s2)
    else:
        arr = np.full(arr.size, target / float(arr.size))
    return arr
```
If target=1 and arr size large, values small. Good.

#### `_convolution(a)`
```
def _convolution(a):
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = arr.size
    if n == 0:
       return np.array([0.0])
    if n < 384:
       c = np.convolve(arr, arr)
       if c.size:
          cmax = float(c.max())
          if cmax > 0 and np.isfinite(cmax):
             tol = 1e-12 * cmax
             # set tiny negatives and values below tol? Not below for legitimate small. Only negative.
             c = np.where(c < 0.0, np.zeros_like(c), c)
       return c
    m = 2*n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < m: N <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(arr, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    c = c[:m]
    # Remove tiny negative
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c
```
Setting negative to 0. If all zeros, c remains zeros.

#### `_score_from_parts(a, c)`
```
def _score_from_parts(a, c):
    n = a.size
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return float('inf')
    if c.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    mb = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return float('inf')
    if mb < 0: mb = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * float(n) * mb / (s * s))
```

#### `_score_internal(a)`
```
def _score_internal(a):
    arr = _normalize(a, 1.0)
    c = _convolution(arr)
    return _score_from_parts(arr,c)
```
But for optimization, avoid re-normalizing each call. Use parts.

#### `_true_score(a)`
```
def _true_score(a):
    arr = _clean_array(a)
    if arr is None or arr.size == 0:
       return float('inf')
    # ensure sum >=0.01? If not, scale? But evaluate would return inf. We can normalize before call for candidate? The evaluator is scale-invariant, but if sum<0.01, inf. We always pass sum=1. If something tiny, scale to 1.
    if arr.sum() < 0.01:
       arr = _normalize(arr, 1.0)
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in arr.tolist()]
        res = evaluate_sequence(vals)
        if res is None:
            return float('inf')
        res = float(res)
        if np.isfinite(res):
            return res
        return float('inf')
    except Exception:
        c = _convolution(arr)
        return _score_from_parts(arr,c)
```
This calls global. If not defined, NameError caught? In except, NameError? Actually calling evaluate_sequence if not defined raises NameError, caught by Exception? NameError is Exception, yes. Good.

#### `_hard_gradient(a, mstar)`
```
def _hard_gradient(a, mstar):
    n = a.size
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    lo = max(0, mstar - n + 1)
    hi = min(n - 1, mstar)
    if lo <= hi:
        idx = np.arange(lo, hi + 1)
        grad[idx] = 2.0 * a[mstar - idx]
    return grad
```

#### `_gradient_from_weights(a, w)`
Use FFT threshold maybe n<160 direct. But direct loop with dot each i O(n^2). For n=160 okay. For n=384 maybe 147k, maybe okay. Use threshold 128.
```
def _gradient_from_weights(a, w):
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.array([])
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([2.0 * float(w[0]) * float(a[0])])
    if n < 128:
       grad = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
       for i in range(n):
           grad[i] = 2.0 * float(np.dot(a, w[i:i+n]))
       return grad
    m = len(w) + n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < m: N <<= 1
    r = a[::-1].copy()
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    fr = np.fft.rfft(r, N)
    z = np.fft.irfft(fw * fr, N)
    grad = 2.0 * z[n-1:2*n-1]
    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return grad
```
Check w length should be 2n-1. If not, m adjust. Good.

#### `_weights_from_conv(c, tau, top_k)`
Dimensionless.
```
def _weights_from_conv(c, tau, top_k):
    L = c.size
    if L == 0: return np.array([1.0])
    if top_k is None:
       top_k = L
    top_k = int(max(0, min(L, top_k)))
    if top_k <= 1:
       w = np.zeros(L)
       w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
       return w
    if top_k == L:
       vals = c
       idx = None
    else:
       # If top_k small, use argpartition
       if top_k < L:
          # np.argpartition requires kth int. Use L-top_k
          idx = np.argpartition(c, L - top_k)[-top_k:]
       else: idx = np.arange(L)
       vals = c[idx]
    vmax = float(np.max(vals))
    if not np.isfinite(vmax) or vmax <= 0:
       w = np.zeros(L); w[int(np.argmax(c))]=1 if L else 0; return w
    # dimensionless x = tau * (vals / vmax - 1) in [-inf? 0]
    x = tau * (vals / vmax - 1.0)
    # clip x to [-50,0] to avoid underflow, but underflow okay maybe; clip prevents exp overflow none.
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0:
       if idx is None:
          e2 = np.zeros(L); e2[int(np.argmax(c))]=1; e=e2; s=1
       else:
          e2 = np.zeros_like(e); e2[int(np.argmax(vals))]=1; e=e2; s=1
    wloc = e / s
    if idx is None:
       return wloc
    w = np.zeros(L)
    w[idx] = wloc
    return w
```
If top_k > L set L. For top_k=0? Use all? We'll treat top_k<=1 hard; top_k=0 maybe all? In schedule use 0 to mean all. Adjust: if top_k == 0 -> all. Let's define:
- top_k = 0: all lags
- top_k = 1: hard
- top_k >1: top k.
In function:
```
if top_k == 0: idx=None; top_k=L
elif top_k ==1: hard
elif top_k >1: top
```
Good.

#### `_make_step(a, c, step, tau, top_k)`
Compute gradient and updated a.
```
def _make_step(a, c, step, tau, top_k):
    n = a.size
    if top_k == 1:
        mstar = int(np.argmax(c))
        g = _hard_gradient(a, mstar)
    else:
        w = _weights_from_conv(c, tau, top_k)
        g = _gradient_from_weights(a, w)
    g = g - float(np.mean(g))
    gscale = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
    if gscale <= 1e-300:
       return a.copy()
    a2 = a - step * (g / gscale)
    # enforce nonnegative; maybe add small floor to avoid all zeros? Use floor = 0.0 then normalize. To allow zeros to reenter, a2 can be positive if g negative. If a2 negative clip 0.
    a2 = np.where(a2 > 0.0, a2, 0.0)
    a2 = _normalize(a2, 1.0)
    return a2
```
But if a has many zeros and gradient for a zero coordinate is zero (because a contributes nothing), g centered may be negative for zero? Gradient for zero coordinate may be positive if there are weights and other a_j? Wait grad_i = 2 sum_j a_j w_{i+j}. If a_i=0, grad_i may still positive due to other j and w at i+j. So centered can be negative or positive. Additive can make a_i positive if grad_i below mean. Good.

#### `_optimize_subgrad(a0, t_limit, mode, rng, tau_start, tau_end, topk_cycle, oracle_every)`
We can combine all.

Parameters mode:
- 'hard': topk_cycle=(1,), tau irrelevant.
- 'soft': topk_cycle=(0,) (all), tau schedule.
- 'mixed': cycle (0, 4, 8, 16, 32, 1), tau schedule.
Better to pass `topk_cycle` directly.

```
def _optimize_subgrad(a0, t_limit, topk_cycle=(0, 8, 16, 32), tau_start=30.0, tau_end=250.0, rng=None, oracle_every=0):
    a = _normalize(a0, 1.0)
    c = _convolution(a)
    cur = _score_from_parts(a,c)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score = cur
    best_true = _true_score(a)  # maybe expensive but once
    end = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(t_limit))
    max_block = max(1, min(2000, int(t_limit*10)))? We can just use it count.
    min_step = 0.01; max_step = 0.35
    plateau = 0
    last_best_score = best_score
    it = 0
    # To avoid infinite, while time
    while time.monotonic() < end - 1.0:
        it += 1
        # if no internal improvement for 1000 and step small, perturb
        frac = min(1.0, it / 2000.0)
        step = max_step * (1.0 - 0.8*frac) + min_step
        if topk_cycle:
           topk = topk_cycle[(it // 200) % len(topk_cycle)]
        else: topk = 0
        tau = tau_start + (tau_end - tau_start)*frac
        if rng is not None and it % 1000 == 0:
           # perturb best? Add noise to current
           noise = rng.random(a.size) * 0.02
           a = _normalize(a + noise, 1.0)
           c = _convolution(a)
           cur = _score_from_parts(a,c)
        a2 = _make_step(a, c, step, tau, topk)
        c2 = _convolution(a2)
        s2 = _score_from_parts(a2,c2)
        if not np.isfinite(s2):
           a2 = a; c2=c; s2=cur
        # If s2 significantly worse and step > min_step, try half (one retry)
        if s2 > cur + 1e-12 and s2 > best_score * 1.2 + 1e-12 and step > min_step*1.5:
           a_half = _make_step(a, c, 0.5*step, tau, topk)
           c_half = _convolution(a_half)
           s_half = _score_from_parts(a_half,c_half)
           if np.isfinite(s_half) and s_half < s2:
               a2,c2,s2 = a_half,c_half,s_half
        a,c,cur = a2,c2,s2
        if cur < best_score - 1e-10:
           best_score = cur; best_a = a.copy(); plateau = 0
        else:
           plateau += 1
        # internal periodic? if plateau high and step low, perturb toward uniform or best
        if plateau > 1500 and rng is not None:
           mix = rng.random()
           a = _normalize((1-mix)*a + mix*best_a + 0.01*rng.random(a.size), 1.0)
           c = _convolution(a); cur = _score_from_parts(a,c)
           plateau=0
        if oracle_every > 0 and it % oracle_every == 0:
           ts = _true_score(best_a)
           if np.isfinite(ts) and ts < best_true:
               best_true = ts
    if oracle_every > 0:
       ts = _true_score(best_a)
       if ts < best_true: best_true = ts
    return best_a, best_score, best_true
```

#### Candidate ranking and refinement schedule
We'll evaluate all starts true, sort. Then:
1. For top starts with n <= 1536, run `_optimize_subgrad_oracle` with slices. This directly minimizes true evaluator.
2. For larger or if time, run internal optimization (faster) on some starts, then evaluate.

- Build sorted starts by true score (lower better). But if exact all uniform 2 and random >2; top many uniform maybe tie. We don't want optimize all uniforms. Use diversity: keep only one per length and type? Simpler, top 12.
- For each candidate, compute slice:
   ```
   remaining = deadline - now

   if n <= 256: base=90

   elif n <=1024: base=210
   else: base=120
   # top rank bonus: if rank 0 base *=1.5
   slice = min(base, remaining - 120)
   if slice < 20: continue
   # choose oracle eval frequency:
   if n <= 256: eval_every=1
   elif n <=512: eval_every=1
   elif n <=1024: eval_every=2
   else: eval_every=5
   arr2, true2 = _optimize_subgrad_oracle(arr, slice, eval_every=eval_every, ...)
   update global
   ```

Implement simplex projection:
Given v (real), find smallest t such that sum(max(v - t,0)) = 1. Sort v descending. Standard:
```
def _project_simplex(v):
    n = v.size
    if n == 0: return v
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    rho = np.nonzero(u * np.arange(1, n+1) > (cssv - 1.0))[0][0]
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1.0) / (rho + 1.0)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
```
But requires sum target 1. Works. If v finite. If all zero? u=0, condition 0*(i) > -1 True at i=0? cssv[0]-1=-1, u0*1 > -1 true, theta=(0-1)/1=-1, max(v-theta)=1, sum n? I recall formula for projection onto probability simplex sum=1: `rho` largest i satisfying `u_i - (cssv_i - 1)/i > 0`? Let's derive.

`_project_simplex` code:
```
def _project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = v.size
    if n == 0: return v
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
       v = np.where(np.isfinite(v), v, 0.0)
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    # use target
    cond = u > (cssv - target) / np.arange(1, n+1)
    idx = np.nonzero(cond)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        # fallback uniform?
        return np.full(n, target/n)
    rho = int(idx[-1])
    theta = (cssv[rho] - target) / (rho + 1.0)
    w = np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
    s = w.sum()
    if s <= 0 or not finite: return np.full(n, target/n)
    if abs(s-target) > 1e-9:
        w *= target/s
    return w
```
This projects to sum target. For v all zero target1: cond true all, theta=-1/n, w=1/n sum1. Good. For v has large positives, works.

Then `_make_step_projected`:
```
v = a - step * (g/gscale)
a2 = _project_simplex(v, 1.0)
a2 = np.minimum(a2, 1000.0) # won't exceed if sum1 and n>=1? if n=1, a2=1
return a2
```
This can produce zeros, but if v for zero coordinate negative, stays zero; if v positive, reenters. Good.

Use projection in `_make_step`. Need ensure for n large, sorting overhead. We can implement `_project_simplex` with `np.sort`; for n=4096 and 1000 iterations maybe 4096 log = ~50k comparisons C, okay. Actually sort 4096 floats ~0.0001s? 1000=0.1s. Fine.

### Need practical optimization
We can implement a robust optimizer. Let's improve previous in specific ways:
1. **Use true objective for line search**: Each step compute actual score (using internal formula equals evaluate) and accept only if improves. This avoids oscillation and can finely tune.
2. **Use curvature-ish step size**: Subgradient of max can be noisy. Use step sizes decreasing slowly. Try step sizes [0.5,0.3,0.1,0.05,0.02] and choose best via line search. This can improve.
3. **Use active peak set (top-k softmax)**: Instead of single max, smooth over top peaks to reduce multiple high lags. We can cycle top-k e.g. [1,2,4,8,16,32,64, all]. Use temperature schedule.
4. **Use simplex projection** (done) for exact constraint.
5. **Multiple lengths and restarts**, including from `height_sequence_1`, and **delayed acceptance/simulated annealing** to escape local minima.
6. **Polish with coordinate descent on active peak**: For peak lag m, we can explicitly reduce c_m by moving mass between coordinates i and n-1-m-i? Let's derive.

### Final program structure
We need output only code. Let's write a comprehensive program with top-level functions.

Detailed final code plan:

```
"""
Searches for non-negative coefficient sequences minimizing the provided evaluator.
Uses FFT-based convolutions, projected subgradient (simplex projection),
adaptive softmax/top-k peak weights, multiple starts including linear ramps and
height_sequence_1, optional LP best-response, and final true-evaluator line search.
"""
import numpy as np
import time
try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None

INF = float('inf')
```
No lambda.

Helper `_clean_array`, `_normalize`, `_uniform`, `_ramp_seq`, `_structured_starts`, `_score_from_parts`, `_convolution`, `_score_internal`, `_true_score`, `_weights_from_conv`, `_hard_gradient`, `_gradient_from_weights`, `_project_simplex`, `_make_step`, `_optimize_subgrad`, `_lp_refine`, `_pool_key`, `_add_total_start`? Need no nested.

`_optimize_subgrad` with parameters. Let's refine it to be efficient and robust.

#### `_optimize_subgrad(a0, t_limit, topk_cycle, tau_start, tau_end, rng, use_true, eval_every, lower_stop)
Input a0 array. It can optimize using internal acceptance and optional true checks. But maybe simpler: use internal acceptance, then caller evaluates true. For true-guided, maybe another wrapper.

Instead of complex flags, implement `_optimize_subgrad` that accepts `accept_mode`:
- 0: accept based on internal score (fast).
- 1: accept based on true score (`_true_score`) every step or every `eval_every`? If true, we evaluate candidate and try half step. This is slower but aligned. Use for small n top candidates.
- 2: mixed: internal for steps but true check every K to update best; maybe not needed.

To keep code manageable, implement two functions: `_optimize_internal` and `_optimize_true`. Both share `_make_step` and schedule. But duplication okay.

`_optimize_internal(a0, t_limit, topk_cycle, tau_start, tau_end, rng)`:
- a = normalize; c; cur; best_a,best_score.
- plateau=0; it=0; end=t_limit.
- max_step=0.4, min_step=0.02.
- For each step until time:
   - if `it > 1000` and `best_score <= 2.0?` Wait lower bound unknown. For full conv ramp 1.33. Lower bound maybe1. We shouldn't stop at2. If best_score close to theoretical 1? Can't know. Use plateau stop.
   - schedule: frac = min(1.0, it / max(1, int(t_limit*100)))? We don't know iters. Use `frac = min(1.0, 0.002 * it / max(1, n?)`? Better schedule based on elapsed time: `progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-t0)/max(1.0,t_limit))`. step = max_step*(1-0.8*progress)+min_step. topk cycle by it//200. tau = tau_start + (tau_end-tau_start)*progress.
   - If plateau > 2000 and progress>0.5, perturb: a = normalize((0.9*a +0.1*best_a + 0.02*rng.random)); c cur.
   - make step; new c/s; if new finite and (s < cur or s < best_score) accept; else if s worse and step large, half step; if half better accept else keep old.
   - Update best if s < best_score.
- return best_a,best_score.

For true `_optimize_true`:
- current `cur_true = _true_score(a)` (could be inf if invalid; fallback). best_true.
- Each iteration:
   - compute c internal for gradient (we might already have from previous? Need compute at start and after accepted. If rejected, current c remains.)
   - make candidate a2; evaluate `s_true = _true_score(a2)`. If not finite, rollback. If s_true < cur_true: accept and recompute c internal (needed for next gradient). If worse, try half candidate `ahalf`; evaluate; if half better, accept; else plateau.
   - To reduce true evals, if `eval_every>1`, for steps not requiring true? But true acceptance requires true. We can set eval_every=1 for true mode. For n<=512 okay. For n 1024 maybe use internal mode. Simpler.
   - If plateau high, perturb current a using rng and recompute true? Perturb may be worse; use if improves? We can evaluate perturbed and accept if improve; else keep. But random perturb might waste. Use small mix with best_a.
   - Stop if plateau > 3000 and progress>0.7.
- Return best_a,best_true.

But true mode one true eval per candidate and maybe half (2 evals). For slice 120s n=512, maybe thousands evals, okay. For n=1024 maybe 1k evals, maybe several seconds. We can use eval_every=1 only for n<=768; for n>768 use internal mode.

#### `_generate_starts(rng, hs, max_n)`

- Quadratic: `np.arange(1,n+1)**2` maybe.

Use `_add_start(starts, arr)` top-level? It would append if finite. No nested. Define:
```
def _append_start(list_starts, arr):
    arr = _clean_array(arr)
    if arr is None or arr.size < 2: return
    if not np.any(np.isfinite(arr)): return
    if float(arr.sum()) <= 1e-300: return
    list_starts.append(arr)
```
It mutates list.

For each L, add:
```
i=np.arange(L, dtype=float)
_append_start(starts, np.full(L,1.0))
_append_start(starts, i+1)
_append_start(starts, L-i)
_append_start(starts, (i+1)**2)
_append_start(starts, np.exp(np.linspace(0, 1.5, L)))
_append_start(starts, np.exp(np.linspace(1.5,0,L)))

#### Time slices in propose
After generating starts, we can do:
1. Evaluate true score for each start; store in list of tuples (true, internal, arr, is_mandatory maybe). But true evaluating all 60 with n=2048 could be 20*4M=80M, okay. Use time check; if budget small, limit.
2. Sort by true score. But if true not computed for all due time, fill internal.
3. Optimize top starts.

A top-level `_pool_key(item): return (item[0], item[1], len(item[2]))`.

Optimization schedule:
```
remaining = deadline - now
for rank, item in enumerate(pool):
   if remaining < 90: break
   arr = item[2]; n=len(arr); true_start=item[0]; internal=item[1]
   # slice based on n and rank
   if n <= 64: slice=45
   elif n <=128: slice=60
   elif n <=256: slice=90
   elif n <=512: slice=150
   elif n <=1024: slice=180
   else: slice=120
   slice = min(slice, remaining - 90)
   if rank == 0: slice = min(slice*1.5, remaining - 90)
   if slice < 20: continue
   # choose true mode if n <= 768 and slice >=60; else internal
   if n <= 768 and true_start < best_true + 1.0: # promising
       new_arr, new_true = _optimize_true(arr, slice, ...)
       evaluate true; update
   else:
       new_arr, new_internal = _optimize_internal(arr, slice,...)
       new_true = _true_score(new_arr); update
   # Optional LP if n<=512 and rank<3 and remaining>60: arr_lp = _lp_refine(arr or new_arr, min(60, remaining-90)); update
```
But `_optimize_true` may duplicate true evaluation of initial (it evaluates). We can pass initial_true to avoid. Modify signature `_optimize_true(a0, t_limit, ..., initial_true=None)`. If None, compute. For internal, no.

Maybe we should always use true mode for top 3 promising and n <=1024. True mode directly optimizes evaluator. For exact/full evaluator, true and internal align. For hidden, true aligns. Use true mode if n <= 1024? True eval 1M, slice 180s. How many evals? Maybe 1000-3000; 1000*1M=1e9 operations, maybe 2-5s? Actually higher but maybe okay. Python loop in eval overhead 1000*1024=1M checks, okay. Could do many. Let's use true mode for n<=1024 and top 5, slice moderate. For larger, internal.

But true mode with line search may be slow if many rejections: For each iteration, at least one `_true_score` call, maybe two if half. For n=1024, `_true_score` direct conv 1M multiplications. If 2000 iterations, 2e9 multiplications; could be 10-20s, okay. 2400 budget, fine. But if `_true_score` also does Python type checks 1024*2000=2M, okay. So true mode feasible.

For n=2048, true eval 4M; 2000 ->8e9, too slow. Use internal.

In `_optimize_true`, to avoid recomputing internal c every rejected step, if candidate rejected, current remains, c remains. If accepted, recompute c. Good. For half step, if rejected, no recompute for a (current). If accepted, recompute for half.

#### `_optimize_true` details
```
def _optimize_true(a0, t_limit, topk_cycle, tau_start, tau_end, rng, initial_true=None):
    a = _normalize(a0,1.0)
    c = _convolution(a)
    cur_internal = _score_from_parts(a,c)
    if initial_true is None: cur_true = _true_score(a)
    else: cur_true = initial_true
    if not finite cur_true: cur_true = cur_internal; # fallback
    best_a = a.copy(); best_true = cur_true; best_internal=cur_internal
    end = time.monotonic()+t_limit; start_t=time.monotonic()
    min_step=0.02; max_step=0.35; plateau=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < end-1:
       if not finite cur_true: break? set to internal.
       it+=1
       progress = min(1.0, (time.monotonic()-start_t)/max(1.0,t_limit))
       step = max_step*(1-0.8*progress)+min_step
       topk = topk_cycle[(it//150)%len(topk_cycle)]
       tau = tau_start + (tau_end-tau_start)*progress
       # if plateau, perturb
       if rng is not None and plateau > 1000 and step < max_step*0.4:
           mix = rng.random()
           noise = 0.05*rng.random(a.size)
           a_new = (1-mix)*a + mix*best_a + noise
           a_new = _normalize(a_new,1.0)
           ts = _true_score(a_new)
           if finite and ts < cur_true - 1e-12:
               a = a_new; c=_convolution(a); cur_internal=...; cur_true=ts; plateau=0
           else:
               # maybe still accept as exploration if ts < best_true? no
               plateau = 0? to avoid repeated perturb every iter; set plateau=1? 
       a2 = _make_step(a,c,step,tau,topk)
       s2 = _true_score(a2)
       accepted=False
       if finite(s2) and s2 < cur_true - 1e-12:
           a=a2; c=_convolution(a); cur_internal=...; cur_true=s2; plateau=0; accepted=True
       else:
           # half step
           ah = _make_step(a,c,0.5*step,tau,topk)
           sh = _true_score(ah)
           if finite(sh) and sh < cur_true - 1e-12:
              a=ah; c=...; cur_true=sh; plateau=0; accepted=True
           else:
              plateau += 1
       if cur_true < best_true - 1e-12:
           best_true=cur_true; best_a=a.copy()
       # stopping: if plateau huge and progress high
       if plateau > 2500 and progress > 0.7:
           break
    # final maybe evaluate best_a true
    best_true = min(best_true, _true_score(best_a))
    return best_a, best_true
```

```
def _lp_refine(a, t_limit, rng=None):
    if linprog is None: return a
    arr = _normalize(a,1.0)
    n=arr.size
    if n > 1024 or t_limit < 5: return arr
    best_a=arr.copy(); best_score=_score_internal(arr)
    best_true=_true_score(arr)
    end = time.monotonic()+t_limit
    for it in range(3):
       if time > end-3: break
       c=_convolution(best_a); M=float(c.max())
       if M <=0: break
       A = np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=float)
       for j in range(n): A[j:j+n,j] = best_a
       b = np.full(2*n-1, M)
       cc = -np.ones(n)
       bounds = [(0.0, None)]*n
       try:
          res = linprog(cc, A_ub=A, b_ub=b, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options={'presolve': True})
          if res.status !=0 or res.x is None: break
          g = _normalize(res.x,1.0)
       except Exception: break
       # test blends
       improved=False
       for t in [0.2,0.5,0.8,0.9,0.3]:
          cand = _normalize((1-t)*best_a + t*g,1.0)
          s = _score_internal(cand)
          if s < best_score - 1e-10:
             best_a=cand; best_score=s; improved=True
       if not improved: break
    # final best true maybe
    ts=_true_score(best_a); ts0=_true_score(_normalize(a,1.0))? We can just return best_a
    return best_a
```

def _generate_starts(rng, hs, max_n=2048, max_starts=72):
    starts=[]
    hs_clean = _clean_array(hs) if hs is not None else None
    if hs_clean is not None and hs_clean.size >=2:
       for L in (128,256,512,1024):
          if L <= hs_clean.size:
             _append_start(starts, hs_clean[:L])
             _append_start(starts, hs_clean[:L][::-1])
       if hs_clean.size <= max_n:
          _append_start(starts, hs_clean)
          _append_start(starts, hs_clean[::-1])
    base_lengths=[]
    for L in (64,128,256,512,1024,2048,4096):
       if L <= max_n:
          base_lengths.append(L)
    # core shapes for all lengths maybe
    for L in base_lengths:
       if len(starts) >= max_starts: break
       i=np.arange(L, dtype=np.float64)
       _append_start(starts, np.full(L,1.0))
       _append_start(starts, i+1.0)
       _append_start(starts, L - i)
    # if more space, add diverse for some lengths
    diverse_lengths = [L for L in base_lengths if L <= max_n and L <=1024]
    for L in diverse_lengths:
       if len(starts) >= max_starts: break
       i=np.arange(L, dtype=np.float64)
       _append_start(starts, (i+1.0)**2)
       _append_start(starts, np.exp(np.linspace(0,1.5,L)))
       _append_start(starts, np.exp(np.linspace(1.5,0,L)))
       tri = np.minimum(i+1.0, L-i) + 0.5
       _append_start(starts, tri)
       _append_start(starts, 0.05 + np.sin(2.0*np.pi*i*3.0/L)**2)
       _append_start(starts, 0.05 + np.sin(np.pi*i/max(1,L-1))**2)
    return starts
```

#### Detailed code for `_optimize_true`
```
def _optimize_true(a0, t_limit, topk_cycle, tau_start, tau_end, rng, initial_true=None):
    a = _normalize(a0, 1.0)
    c = _convolution(a)
    if initial_true is None:
        cur_true = _true_score(a)
    else:
        cur_true = float(initial_true)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_true):
        cur_true = _score_internal(a)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_true):
        return a, cur_true
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_true = cur_true
    if t_limit <= 0:
        return best_a, best_true
    start_t = time.monotonic()
    end_t = start_t + max(1.0, float(t_limit))
    min_step = 0.02
    max_step = 0.5
    plateau = 0
    it = 0
    while time.monotonic() < end_t - 1.0:
        it += 1
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start_t
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1.0, float(t_limit)))
        step = max_step * (1.0 - 0.8 * progress) + min_step
        if topk_cycle:
            topk = topk_cycle[(it // 150) % len(topk_cycle)]
        else:
            topk = 1
        tau = tau_start + (tau_end - tau_start) * progress
        if rng is not None and plateau > 500 and progress > 0.3:
            mix = float(rng.random())
            noise_scale = 0.02 + 0.03 * float(rng.random())
            cand = (1.0 - mix) * a + mix * best_a + noise_scale * rng.random(a.size)
            cand = _normalize(cand, 1.0)
            ts = _true_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(ts) and ts < cur_true - 1e-12:
                a = cand
                c = _convolution(a)
                cur_true = ts
                plateau = 0
            else:
                plateau = 0
        a2 = _make_step(a, c, step, tau, topk)
        s2 = _true_score(a2)
        accepted = False
        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < cur_true - 1e-12:
            a = a2
            c = _convolution(a)
            cur_true = s2
            plateau = 0
            accepted = True
        if not accepted:
            if step > min_step * 1.2:
                ah = _make_step(a, c, 0.5 * step, tau, topk)
                sh = _true_score(ah)
                if np.isfinite(sh) and sh < cur_true - 1e-12:
                    a = ah
                    c = _convolution(a)
                    cur_true = sh
                    plateau = 0
                    accepted = True
            if not accepted:
                plateau += 1
        if cur_true < best_true - 1e-12:
            best_true = cur_true
            best_a = a.copy()
        if plateau > 4000 and progress > 0.75:
            break
    ts = _true_score(best_a)
    if np.isfinite(ts) and ts < best_true:
        best_true = ts
    return best_a, best_true
```
If no topk_cycle, use 1. Good.

`_score_internal` in fallback may compute conv direct/FFT.

#### `_optimize_internal`

```
def _optimize_internal(a0, t_limit, topk_cycle, tau_start, tau_end, rng):
   a=normalize; c=conv; cur=score; best...
   while...
      step...
      a2=_make_step; c2=conv; s2=score
      if finite and s2 < cur -1e-12: accept
      else half: ah, ch, sh; if sh < cur and sh < s2? accept if sh < cur? Actually if half better than current accept; if not, keep.
      if accepted cur... plateau0 else plateau+
      if s < best update (use cur after accept)
   return
```
For half, if sh < cur - 1e-12 accept. If half not better, maybe no accept. If s2 is worse but less than best? It cannot be less than best if current not? But could current > best and half < best but >cur? Actually half < cur? use cur.
Acceptance condition should be candidate improves current (line search), not just best. Good.

Perturb in internal mode similar: if plateau>1000, mix and evaluate internal; accept if improves current.
Stop if plateau>8000 and progress>0.8.

### `propose_candidate` detailed code
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + max(10.0, budget - 10.0)
    max_n = 1024
    if budget > 1500: max_n = 2048
    hs = globals().get('height_sequence_1', None)
    hs_clean = _clean_array(hs) if hs is not None else None
    starts = _generate_starts(rng, hs_clean, max_n, max_starts=64)
    pool=[]
    best_seq=None; best_true=INF
    for arr in starts:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30: break
       a = _normalize(arr,1.0)
       inner = _score_internal(a)
       true = _true_score(a)
       pool.append((true, inner, a))
       if np.isfinite(true) and true < best_true:
          best_true=true; best_seq=a.copy()
    if not pool:
       a = np.arange(1,513); pool.append((INF, INF, a))?
    pool.sort(key=_pool_key)
    print(...)
    cycle_true=(1,8,3,16,64)
    cycle_internal=(0,8,16,32,1)
    true_count=0
    for rank, item in enumerate(pool):
       now=time.monotonic()
       remaining = deadline - now
       if remaining < 120: break
       true_start=item[0]; inner=item[1]; a=item[2]
       if not np.isfinite(true_start): true_start=inner if np.isfinite(inner) else best_true+10
       n=len(a)
       base=60
       if n <=64: base=30
       elif n <=128: base=60
       elif n <=256: base=90
       elif n <=512: base=150
       elif n <=1024: base=180
       else: base=120
       if rank ==0: base = min(base + 120, int(remaining*0.8))
       slice_s = min(base, remaining - 120)
       if slice_s < 25: continue
       use_true = (n <= 1024 and true_count < 5 and np.isfinite(true_start) and slice_s >= 45)
       if use_true:
          true_count +=1
          new_arr, new_true = _optimize_true(a, slice_s, cycle_true, 30.0, 250.0, rng, initial_true=true_start)
          if not np.isfinite(new_true): new_true = _true_score(new_arr)
       else:
          new_arr, _ = _optimize_internal(a, slice_s, cycle_internal, 30.0, 250.0, rng)
          new_true = _true_score(new_arr)
       if np.isfinite(new_true) and new_true < best_true - 1e-12:
          best_true=new_true; best_seq=new_arr.copy(); print
       else:
          print no improvement
    # LP
    if best_seq is not None and np.isfinite(best_true) and best_true > 1.45 and len(best_seq) <= 800:
       remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
       if remaining > 90:
          lp_arr = _lp_refine(best_seq, min(120.0, remaining - 40.0), rng)
          lp_true = _true_score(lp_arr)
          if finite and < best: update
    # extension
    if best_seq is not None:
       remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
       if remaining > 200 and len(best_seq) * 2 <= max_n:
          ext = _upsample(best_seq, min(len(best_seq)*2, max_n))
          ext_true = _true_score(ext)
          if finite and ext_true < best_true:
             best_true=ext_true; best_seq=ext.copy(); print
          # optimize ext with smaller slice
          ext_n=len(ext)
          slice_ext = min(180.0, remaining - 60.0)
          if slice_ext > 30:
             if ext_n <=1024 and true_count < 7: ... _optimize_true slice_ext
             else internal
             update
    # final refinement on best
    if best_seq is not None:
       remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
       if remaining > 60:
          n=len(best_seq)
          if n <= 1024 and true_count < 8:
             final = _optimize_true(best_seq, remaining-20, cycle_true, 50, 350, rng, initial_true=best_true)
          else:
             final,_ = _optimize_internal(best_seq, remaining-20, cycle_internal, 50,350,rng)
          true_final=_true_score(final)
          update
    # sanity final fallback
    if best_seq is None:
       candidates=[]
       if hs_clean is not None and hs_clean.size>=2: candidates.append(hs_clean)
       candidates.append(np.arange(1,513))
       candidates.append(np.ones(512))
       chosen=None; chosen_score=INF
       for cand in candidates:
          a=_normalize(cand,1.0); s=_true_score(a)
          if finite and s<chosen_score: chosen_score=s; chosen=a
       best_seq=chosen if chosen is not None else np.arange(1,513)
    best_seq=_normalize(best_seq,1.0)
    final_score=_true_score(best_seq)
    if not np.isfinite(final_score): fallback uniform
    print
    return [float(x) for x in best_seq]
```

### Let's refine `_generate_starts` for max_n and shapes
```
def _generate_starts(rng, hs, max_n=2048, max_starts=64):
    starts=[]
    if hs is not None and hs.size >= 2:
       for L in (128,256,512,1024):
          if L <= hs.size and len(starts) < max_starts:
             _append_start(starts, hs[:L])
             _append_start(starts, hs[:L][::-1])
       if hs.size <= max_n and len(starts) < max_starts:
          _append_start(starts, hs)
          _append_start(starts, hs[::-1])
    base_lengths=[]
    for L in (64,128,256,512,1024,2048):
       if L <= max_n: base_lengths.append(L)
    # core: uniform, inc, dec for each length
    for L in base_lengths:
       if len(starts) >= max_starts: break
       i=np.arange(L, dtype=np.float64)
       _append_start(starts, np.full(L,1.0))
       _append_start(starts, i + 1.0)
       _append_start(starts, L - i)
    # diverse for lengths <= min(max_n,1024)
    for L in base_lengths:
       if len(starts) >= max_starts: break
       if L > 1024: continue
       i=np.arange(L, dtype=np.float64)
       _append_start(starts, np.power(i + 1.0, 0.5))
       _append_start(starts, np.power(i + 1.0, 2.0))
       _append_start(starts, np.exp(np.linspace(0.0, 1.8, L)))
       tri = np.minimum(i + 1.0, L - i) + 0.25
       _append_start(starts, tri)
       x = i / max(1, L - 1)
       edge = np.exp(-12.0 * x * x) + np.exp(-12.0 * (1.0 - x) * (1.0 - x)) + 0.01
       _append_start(starts, edge)
       _append_start(starts, 0.05 + np.sin(np.pi * x) ** 2)
       _append_start(starts, 0.05 + np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x * 2.0) ** 2)
    return starts
```

```python
"""
Searches for non-negative coefficient sequences minimizing the provided
evaluate_sequence objective.

The optimizer combines:
  - FFT/convolution-based peak-convolution scoring,
  - projected subgradient descent on the simplex (non-negative, fixed sum),
  - adaptive hard / soft-max / top-k peak weighting,
  - direct line search against the actual evaluator when the problem size
    permits,
  - multi-start candidates including ramps, powers, Gaussians, sines, edge
    peaks, and the optional previous construction in height_sequence_1,
  - optional LP best-response refinement,
  - length extension by interpolation and final polishing.

The final output is always a plain Python list of finite, non-negative floats.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None

INF = float("inf")


def _clean_array(a):
    """Convert input to a 1D finite non-negative float array, clipped to [0,1000]."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    if arr.ndim != 1:
        arr = arr.reshape(-1)
    if arr.size == 0:
        return arr
    arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)
    return arr


def _normalize(a, target=1.0):
    """Normalize a non-negative array to the desired sum."""
    arr = _clean_array(a)
    if arr is None or arr.size == 0:
        return np.full(1, float(target), dtype=np.float64)

    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300:
        return np.full(arr.size, target / float(arr.size), dtype=np.float64)

    arr = arr * (target / s)
    arr = np.minimum(arr, 1000.0)

    s2 = float(arr.sum())
    if s2 > 1e-300:
        arr = arr * (target / s2)
    else:
        arr = np.full(arr.size, target / float(arr.size), dtype=np.float64)

    return arr


def _uniform(n):
    """Uniform sequence of length n, normalized to sum 1."""
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    return np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)


def _upsample(a, new_n):
    """Linearly resample a sequence to a new length and normalize to sum 1."""
    arr = _normalize(a, 1.0)
    m = arr.size
    if m <= 1 or new_n <= 1:
        return _uniform(max(1, int(new_n)))

    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, int(new_n), dtype=np.float64)
    out = np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)
    return _normalize(out, 1.0)


def _convolution(a):
    """Full convolution a*a, using direct convolution for small n and FFT otherwise."""
    arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = arr.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)

    if n < 128:
        c = np.convolve(arr, arr, mode="full")
        c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
        return c

    m = 2 * n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < m:
        N <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(arr, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    c = c[:m]
    c = np.where(np.isfinite(c), c, 0.0)
    c = np.where(c > 0.0, c, 0.0)
    return c


def _score_from_parts(a, c):
    """Compute the evaluator objective from the array and its convolution."""
    n = a.size
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if s < 0.01:
        return INF
    if c.size == 0:
        return INF
    mb = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return INF
    if mb < 0.0:
        mb = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * float(n) * mb / (s * s))


def _score_internal(a):
    """Fast internal copy of the evaluator objective."""
    arr = _normalize(a, 1.0)
    c = _convolution(arr)
    return _score_from_parts(arr, c)


def _true_score(a):
    """Score using the available evaluate_sequence, falling back to internal."""
    arr = _clean_array(a)
    if arr is None or arr.size == 0:
        return INF
    s = float(arr.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        arr = _normalize(arr, 1.0)
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in arr.tolist()]
        res = evaluate_sequence(vals)
        res = float(res)
        if np.isfinite(res):
            return res
        return INF
    except Exception:
        c = _convolution(arr)
        return _score_from_parts(arr, c)


def _hard_gradient(a, mstar):
    """Subgradient for a single active convolution coefficient."""
    n = a.size
    grad = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 0:
        return grad
    lo = max(0, mstar - n + 1)
    hi = min(n - 1, mstar)
    if lo <= hi:
        idx = np.arange(lo, hi + 1)
        grad[idx] = 2.0 * a[mstar - idx]
    return grad


def _gradient_from_weights(a, w):
    """Gradient of sum_m w_m c_m for c = a*a."""
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([2.0 * float(w[0]) * float(a[0])], dtype=np.float64)

    if n < 128:
        grad = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for i in range(n):
            grad[i] = 2.0 * float(np.dot(a, w[i:i + n]))
        return grad

    m = len(w) + n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < m:
        N <<= 1

    r = a[::-1].copy()
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    fr = np.fft.rfft(r, N)
    z = np.fft.irfft(fw * fr, N)
    grad = 2.0 * z[n - 1:2 * n - 1]
    grad = np.where(np.isfinite(grad), grad, 0.0)
    return grad


def _weights_from_conv(c, tau, top_k):
    """Soft weights over convolution coefficients, with optional top-k restriction."""
    L = c.size
    if L == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if L == 1:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)

    if top_k is None:
        top_k = 0
    top_k = int(top_k)

    if top_k == 1:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w

    if top_k <= 0 or top_k >= L:
        idx = None
        vals = c
    else:
        idx = np.argpartition(c, L - top_k)[-top_k:]
        vals = c[idx]

    vmax = float(np.max(vals))
    if not np.isfinite(vmax) or vmax <= 0.0:
        w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        w[int(np.argmax(c))] = 1.0
        return w

    x = tau * (vals / vmax - 1.0)
    x = np.clip(x, -50.0, 0.0)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        e = np.zeros_like(e, dtype=np.float64)
        e[int(np.argmax(vals))] = 1.0
        s = 1.0

    wloc = e / s
    if idx is None:
        return wloc

    w = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
    w[idx] = wloc
    return w


def _project_simplex(v, target=1.0):
    """Euclidean projection onto {x >= 0, sum(x) = target}."""
    arr = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    n = arr.size
    if n == 0:
        return arr.copy()
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(arr)):
        arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr), arr, 0.0)

    u = np.sort(arr)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    denom = np.arange(1, n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    cond = u > (cssv - target) / denom
    idx = np.nonzero(cond)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        return np.full(n, target / float(n), dtype=np.float64)

    rho = int(idx[-1])
    theta = (cssv[rho] - target) / float(rho + 1)
    out = np.maximum(arr - theta, 0.0)
    s = float(out.sum())
    if s <= 1e-300 or not np.isfinite(s):
        return np.full(n, target / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    if abs(s - target) > 1e-9 * max(1.0, abs(target)):
        out *= target / s
    return out


def _make_step(a, c, step, tau, top_k):
    """One projected subgradient / soft-subgradient step on the simplex."""
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return a.copy()

    top_k = int(top_k)
    if top_k == 1:
        g = _hard_gradient(a, int(np.argmax(c)))
    else:
        w = _weights_from_conv(c, tau, top_k)
        g = _gradient_from_weights(a, w)

    g = g - float(np.mean(g))
    gscale = float(np.max(np.abs(g)))
    if gscale <= 1e-300:
        return a.copy()

    v = a - float(step) * (g / gscale)
    out = _project_simplex(v, 1.0)
    out = np.maximum(out, 0.0)
    out = np.minimum(out, 1000.0)
    return _normalize(out, 1.0)


def _optimize_true(a0, t_limit, topk_cycle, tau_start, tau_end, rng, initial_true=None):
    """
    Projected subgradient refinement where candidate acceptance is judged by
    the actual evaluator.  Used for moderate lengths where repeated evaluator
    calls are affordable.
    """
    a = _normalize(a0, 1.0)
    c = _convolution(a)

    if initial_true is None:
        cur_true = _true_score(a)
    else:
        cur_true = float(initial_true)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_true):
        cur_true = _score_internal(a)
    if not np.isfinite(cur_true):
        return a, INF

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_true = cur_true

    if t_limit <= 0:
        return best_a, best_true

    start_t = time.monotonic()
    end_t = start_t + max(1.0, float(t_limit))
    min_step = 0.02
    max_step = 0.50
    plateau = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end_t - 1.0:
        it += 1
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start_t
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1.0, float(t_limit)))
        step = max_step * (1.0 - 0.8 * progress) + min_step

        if topk_cycle:
            topk = topk_cycle[(it // 150) % len(topk_cycle)]
        else:
            topk = 1

        tau = float(tau_start) + (float(tau_end) - float(tau_start)) * progress

        if rng is not None and plateau > 500 and progress > 0.3:
            mix = float(rng.random())
            noise_scale = 0.02 + 0.03 * float(rng.random())
            cand = (1.0 - mix) * a + mix * best_a + noise_scale * rng.random(a.size)
            cand = _normalize(cand, 1.0)
            ts = _true_score(cand)
            if np.isfinite(ts) and ts < cur_true - 1e-12:
                a = cand
                c = _convolution(a)
                cur_true = ts
            plateau = 0

        a2 = _make_step(a, c, step, tau, topk)
        s2 = _true_score(a2)
        accepted = False

        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < cur_true - 1e-12:
            a = a2
            c = _convolution(a)
            cur_true = s2
            plateau = 0
            accepted = True

        if not accepted and step > min_step * 1.2:
            ah = _make_step(a, c, 0.5 * step, tau, topk)
            sh = _true_score(ah)
            if np.isfinite(sh) and sh < cur_true - 1e-12:
                a = ah
                c = _convolution(a)
                cur_true = sh
                plateau = 0
                accepted = True

        if not accepted:
            plateau += 1

        if cur_true < best_true - 1e-12:
            best_true = cur_true
            best_a = a.copy()

        if plateau > 4000 and progress > 0.75:
            break

    ts = _true_score(best_a)
    if np.isfinite(ts) and ts < best_true:
        best_true = ts

    return best_a, best_true


def _optimize_internal(a0, t_limit, topk_cycle, tau_start, tau_end, rng):
    """
    Fast projected subgradient refinement using the internal FFT-based score.
    """
    a = _normalize(a0, 1.0)
    c = _convolution(a)
    cur = _score_from_parts(a, c)

    if not np.isfinite(cur):
        return a, INF

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = cur

    if t_limit <= 0:
        return best_a, best_score

    start_t = time.monotonic()
    end_t = start_t + max(1.0, float(t_limit))
    min_step = 0.02
    max_step = 0.50
    plateau = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < end_t - 1.0:
        it += 1
        elapsed = time.monotonic() - start_t
        progress = min(1.0, elapsed / max(1.0, float(t_limit)))
        step = max_step * (1.0 - 0.8 * progress) + min_step

        if topk_cycle:
            topk = topk_cycle[(it // 150) % len(topk_cycle)]
        else:
            topk = 1

        tau = float(tau_start) + (float(tau_end) - float(tau_start)) * progress

        if rng is not None and plateau > 800 and progress > 0.3:
            mix = float(rng.random())
            noise_scale = 0.02 + 0.03 * float(rng.random())
            cand = (1.0 - mix) * a + mix * best_a + noise_scale * rng.random(a.size)
            cand = _normalize(cand, 1.0)
            cs = _convolution(cand)
            sc = _score_from_parts(cand, cs)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur - 1e-12:
                a = cand
                c = cs
                cur = sc
            plateau = 0

        a2 = _make_step(a, c, step, tau, topk)
        c2 = _convolution(a2)
        s2 = _score_from_parts(a2, c2)
        accepted = False

        if np.isfinite(s2) and s2 < cur - 1e-12:
            a = a2
            c = c2
            cur = s2
            plateau = 0
            accepted = True

        if not accepted and step > min_step * 1.2:
            ah = _make_step(a, c, 0.5 * step, tau, topk)
            ch = _convolution(ah)
            sh = _score_from_parts(ah, ch)
            if np.isfinite(sh) and sh < cur - 1e-12:
                a = ah
                c = ch
                cur = sh
                plateau = 0
                accepted = True

        if not accepted:
            plateau += 1

        if cur < best_score - 1e-12:
            best_score = cur
            best_a = a.copy()

        if plateau > 8000 and progress > 0.8:
            break

    return best_a, best_score


def _lp_refine(a, t_limit, rng=None):
    """
    Optional LP best-response refinement for small lengths:
      max sum(g) subject to (a*g) <= M, g >= 0.
    Blends are accepted only if the internal objective improves.
    """
    if linprog is None:
        return _normalize(a, 1.0)

    arr = _normalize(a, 1.0)
    n = arr.size
    if n > 1024 or t_limit < 5.0:
        return arr

    best_a = arr.copy()
    best_score = _score_internal(arr)
    start_t = time.monotonic()
    end_t = start_t + max(1.0, float(t_limit))

    for _ in range(4):
        if time.monotonic() > end_t - 3.0:
            break

        c = _convolution(best_a)
        M = float(np.max(c))
        if not np.isfinite(M) or M <= 0.0:
            break
        M = max(M * 1.03, 1e-15)

        A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(n):
            A[j:j + n, j] = best_a

        b = np.full(2 * n - 1, M, dtype=np.float64)
        c_obj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = [(0.0, None)] * n

        try:
            res = linprog(
                c_obj,
                A_ub=A,
                b_ub=b,
                bounds=bounds,
                method="highs",
                options={"presolve": True},
            )
            if res is None or res.status != 0 or res.x is None:
                break
            g = _normalize(res.x, 1.0)
        except Exception:
            break

        cand_best = None
        cand_score = best_score

        for t in (0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.8, 0.9):
            cand = _normalize((1.0 - t) * best_a + t * g, 1.0)
            sc = _score_internal(cand)
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cand_score - 1e-10:
                cand_score = sc
                cand_best = cand

        if cand_best is None:
            break

        best_a = cand_best
        best_score = cand_score

    return best_a


def _append_start(starts, arr):
    """Append a valid start sequence to the list."""
    arr = _clean_array(arr)
    if arr is None or arr.size < 2:
        return
    if not np.any(arr > 0.0):
        return
    starts.append(arr)


def _generate_starts(rng, hs, max_n=2048, max_starts=72):
    """Generate diverse non-negative starting sequences."""
    starts = []

    if hs is not None and hs.size >= 2:
        for L in (128, 256, 512, 1024):
            if L <= hs.size and len(starts) < max_starts:
                _append_start(starts, hs[:L])
                _append_start(starts, hs[:L][::-1])
        if hs.size <= max_n and len(starts) < max_starts:
            _append_start(starts, hs)
            _append_start(starts, hs[::-1])

    base_lengths = []
    for L in (64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048):
        if L <= max_n:
            base_lengths.append(L)

    for L in base_lengths:
        if len(starts) >= max_starts:
            break
        i = np.arange(L, dtype=np.float64)
        _append_start(starts, np.full(L, 1.0, dtype=np.float64))
        _append_start(starts, i + 1.0)
        _append_start(starts, L - i)

    for L in base_lengths:
        if len(starts) >= max_starts:
            break
        if L > 1024:
            continue

        i = np.arange(L, dtype=np.float64)
        x = i / float(max(1, L - 1))

        _append_start(starts, np.power(i + 1.0, 0.5))
        _append_start(starts, (i + 1.0) * 1.0)
        _append_start(starts, (i + 1.0) ** 2)
        _append_start(starts, 1.0 + np.log(i + 1.0))

        _append_start(starts, np.exp(np.linspace(0.0, 1.8, L)))
        _append_start(starts, np.exp(np.linspace(1.8, 0.0, L)))

        tri = np.minimum(i + 1.0, L - i) + 0.25
        _append_start(starts, tri)

        edge = (
            np.exp(-12.0 * x * x)
            + np.exp(-12.0 * (1.0 - x) * (1.0 - x))
            + 0.01
        )
        _append_start(starts, edge)

        _append_start(starts, 0.05 + np.sin(np.pi * x) ** 2)
        _append_start(starts, 0.05 + np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x * 2.0) ** 2)
        _append_start(starts, 0.05 + np.sin(2.0 * np.pi * x * 3.0) ** 2)

    return starts


def _pool_key(item):
    """Sort key for candidate pool: true score, internal score, length."""
    return (item[0], item[1], len(item[2]))


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for the best non-negative coefficient sequence within the time budget.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t0 = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(30.0, float(budget_s))
    deadline = t0 + max(10.0, budget - 10.0)

    max_n = 1024
    if budget > 1500:
        max_n = 2048

    hs_raw = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    hs = _clean_array(hs_raw) if hs_raw is not None else None

    starts = _generate_starts(rng, hs, max_n=max_n, max_starts=72)

    pool = []
    best_seq = None
    best_true = INF

    for arr in starts:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30.0:
            break
        a = _normalize(arr, 1.0)
        inner = _score_internal(a)
        true = _true_score(a)
        pool.append((true, inner, a))

        if np.isfinite(true) and true < best_true:
            best_true = true
            best_seq = a.copy()

    if not pool:
        a = _normalize(np.arange(1, 513.0), 1.0)
        inner = _score_internal(a)
        true = _true_score(a)
        pool.append((true, inner, a))
        if np.isfinite(true) and true < best_true:
            best_true = true
            best_seq = a.copy()

    pool.sort(key=_pool_key)

    print(
        "Initial best: true={:.6f}, n={}".format(
            best_true,
            0 if best_seq is None else len(best_seq),
        ),
        flush=True,
    )

    cycle_true = (1, 8, 3, 16, 64)
    cycle_internal = (0, 8, 16, 32, 1)
    true_mode_count = 0

    for rank, item in enumerate(pool):
        now = time.monotonic()
        remaining = deadline - now
        if remaining < 120.0:
            break

        true_start = item[0]
        inner_start = item[1]
        a = item[2]
        n = len(a)

        if not np.isfinite(true_start):
            true_start = inner_start if np.isfinite(inner_start) else best_true + 10.0
        if not np.isfinite(true_start):
            continue

        if n <= 64:
            base = 30.0
        elif n <= 128:
            base = 60.0
        elif n <= 256:
            base = 90.0
        elif n <= 512:
            base = 150.0
        elif n <= 1024:
            base = 180.0
        else:
            base = 120.0

        if rank == 0:
            base = min(base + 120.0, max(60.0, 0.75 * remaining))
        slice_s = min(base, remaining - 120.0)
        if slice_s < 25.0:
            continue

        use_true = (
            n <= 1024
            and true_mode_count < 6
            and np.isfinite(true_start)
            and slice_s >= 45.0
        )

        if use_true:
            true_mode_count += 1
            new_arr, new_true = _optimize_true(
                a,
                slice_s,
                cycle_true,
                30.0,
                250.0,
                rng,
                initial_true=true_start,
            )
            if not np.isfinite(new_true):
                new_true = _true_score(new_arr)
        else:
            new_arr, _ = _optimize_internal(
                a,
                slice_s,
                cycle_internal,
                30.0,
                250.0,
                rng,
            )
            new_true = _true_score(new_arr)

        if np.isfinite(new_true) and new_true < best_true - 1e-12:
            best_true = new_true
            best_seq = new_arr.copy()
            print(
                "Improved: true={:.6f}, n={}, method={}".format(
                    new_true, len(new_seq if (new_seq := new_arr) else []), "true" if use_true else "internal"
                ),
                flush=True,
            )
        else:
            print(
                "No improvement: rank={}, n={}, best={:.6f}, candidate={:.6f}".format(
                    rank, n, best_true, new_true if np.isfinite(new_true) else INF
                ),
                flush=True,
            )

    # Optional LP refinement when the current best seems close to the target regime.
    if (
        best_seq is not None
        and np.isfinite(best_true)
        and best_true > 1.45
        and len(best_seq) <= 800
    ):
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 90.0:
            lp_arr = _lp_refine(best_seq, min(120.0, remaining - 40.0), rng)
            lp_true = _true_score(lp_arr)
            if np.isfinite(lp_true) and lp_true < best_true - 1e-12:
                best_true = lp_true
                best_seq = lp_arr.copy()
                print("LP improved: true={:.6f}, n={}".format(best_true, len(best_seq)), flush=True)

    # Length extension: longer support can sometimes lower the normalized peak.
    if best_seq is not None:
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 200.0 and len(best_seq) * 2 <= max_n:
            ext_len = min(len(best_seq) * 2, max_n)
            ext = _upsample(best_seq, ext_len)
            ext_true = _true_score(ext)
            if np.isfinite(ext_true) and ext_true < best_true - 1e-12:
                best_true = ext_true
                best_seq = ext.copy()
                print("Extension improved: true={:.6f}, n={}".format(best_true, len(best_seq)), flush=True)

            slice_ext = min(180.0, remaining - 60.0)
            if slice_ext > 30.0 and len(best_seq) <= max_n:
                if len(best_seq) <= 1024 and true_mode_count < 8:
                    new_arr, new_true = _optimize_true(
                        best_seq,
                        slice_ext,
                        cycle_true,
                        40.0,
                        300.0,
                        rng,
                        initial_true=best_true,
                    )
                    true_mode_count += 1
                    if not np.isfinite(new_true):
                        new_true = _true_score(new_arr)
                else:
                    new_arr, _ = _optimize_internal(
                        best_seq,
                        slice_ext,
                        cycle_internal,
                        40.0,
                        300.0,
                        rng,
                    )
                    new_true = _true_score(new_arr)

                if np.isfinite(new_true) and new_true < best_true - 1e-12:
                    best_true = new_true
                    best_seq = new_arr.copy()
                    print(
                        "Extended refinement improved: true={:.6f}, n={}".format(
                            best_true, len(best_seq)
                        ),
                        flush=True,
                    )

    # Final polishing of the current best.
    if best_seq is not None:
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining > 60.0:
            if len(best_seq) <= 1024 and true_mode_count < 9:
                final_arr, final_true = _optimize_true(
                    best_seq,
                    remaining - 20.0,
                    cycle_true,
                    50.0,
                    350.0,
                    rng,
                    initial_true=best_true,
                )
                true_mode_count += 1
                if not np.isfinite(final_true):
                    final_true = _true_score(final_arr)
            else:
                final_arr, _ = _optimize_internal(
                    best_seq,
                    remaining - 20.0,
                    cycle_internal,
                    50.0,
                    350.0,
                    rng,
                )
                final_true = _true_score(final_arr)

            if np.isfinite(final_true) and final_true < best_true - 1e-12:
                best_true = final_true
                best_seq = final_arr.copy()
                print("Final polish improved: true={:.6f}, n={}".format(best_true, len(best_seq)), flush=True)

    # Robust fallback selection.
    if best_seq is None:
        chosen = None
        chosen_score = INF

        candidates = []
        if hs is not None and hs.size >= 2:
            candidates.append(hs)
        candidates.append(np.arange(1.0, 513.0))
        candidates.append(np.ones(512, dtype=np.float64))

        for cand in candidates:
            a = _normalize(cand, 1.0)
            s = _true_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(s) and s < chosen_score:
                chosen_score = s
                chosen = a

        best_seq = chosen if chosen is not None else np.arange(1.0, 513.0)

    best_seq = _normalize(best_seq, 1.0)
    final_score = _true_score(best_seq)

    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        ramp = _normalize(np.arange(1.0, 513.0), 1.0)
        ramp_score = _true_score(ramp)
        if np.isfinite(ramp_score):
            best_seq = ramp
            final_score = ramp_score

    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        best_seq = _uniform(512)
        final_score = _true_score(best_seq)

    print(
        "Final returned: n={}, true_score={:.6f}".format(len(best_seq), final_score),
        flush=True,
    )

    return [float(x) for x in best_seq.tolist()]
```
